Endometrial Cancer

ICD-10: C54.x (malignant neoplasm of corpus uteri) CCW prevalence (2022 Medicare FFS): 1.1% (females only) — rank 29 of 30 Category: Cancer (females only) Primary associations consulted: NCCN, SGO, ACOG, USPSTF, ACS

At-a-glance schedule

Type Every visit Quarterly Every 6 months Annually Every 2–3 years As indicated
Labs — Serum CA-125 only if elevated at baseline / diagnosis (otherwise NOT routine) — — — Universal MMR IHC (± MLH1 methylation reflex) ± MSI on tumor at diagnosis for Lynch syndrome screening; germline genetic counseling / testing if MMR-deficient (not MLH1 methylation) or strong family history; irAE labs (TSH, LFTs, glucose, cortisol) baseline + prior to each cycle of checkpoint inhibitor (pembrolizumab / dostarlimab)
Imaging — H&P + pelvic exam every 3–6 mo × 2–3 yr (stage III/IV or serous / clear cell every 3 mo) H&P + pelvic exam every 6 mo through yr 5 (after initial high-frequency window); CT chest / abdomen / pelvis every 6 mo × 3 yr (stage III-IV, serous / clear cell, carcinosarcoma) — — Vaginal / pelvic MRI or CT as clinically indicated for symptoms or abnormal exam; response imaging CT chest / abd / pelvis every 2–3 cycles (≈ every 9–12 wk) during active systemic therapy for metastatic / recurrent disease
Physical exam — H&P with speculum + bimanual pelvic exam every 3–6 mo first 2–3 yr H&P every 6 mo through yr 5 H&P annually after yr 5; lymphedema surveillance + education (post-pelvic lymphadenectomy / pelvic RT) — —
Functional / surveys Patient education on recurrence symptoms (vaginal bleeding, pelvic / abdominal pain, cough, weight loss, leg swelling); distress / depression / anxiety screening (NCCN Distress Thermometer) — — Lifestyle counseling (weight management, exercise, healthy diet, smoking cessation — endometrial cancer is metabolically driven); cardiovascular risk management (BP, lipids, diabetes screening); vasomotor symptom management (non-hormonal preferred; HT case-by-case); sexual health / vaginal health (moisturizers, dilators after vaginal brachytherapy); bone health (DXA per osteoporosis guidelines, especially if premenopausal at oophorectomy); age-appropriate other-cancer screening (breast, cervical per ACS / USPSTF, colorectal — accelerated to age 20–25 q1–2 yr if Lynch+) — Vaginal cuff cytology / Pap NOT recommended routinely (discontinued in current NCCN — symptom-driven evaluation only)

Summary recommendation

US post-treatment surveillance for endometrial cancer is driven primarily by NCCN Uterine Neoplasms v3.2025 and the 2025 SGO clinical practice statement, which are aligned.12 H&P with speculum and bimanual pelvic exam is performed every 3–6 months for the first 2–3 years and then every 6–12 months through year 5 (annually thereafter), with frequency stratified by risk: stage IA grade 1–2 disease at the lower end (every 6 months) and stage III-IV or serous / clear cell histology every 3 months for 2–3 years. Routine vaginal cuff cytology has been discontinued — it detects <1% of recurrences, is cost-ineffective, and both NCCN and SGO (Choosing Wisely) explicitly recommend against it; evaluation is symptom- and exam-driven. Routine surveillance CT is NOT recommended for low-risk stage I disease but IS recommended every 6 months for years 1–3 in stage III-IV, serous / clear cell, or carcinosarcoma. Serum CA-125 is reserved for patients with elevated baseline levels (TOTEM trial showed no benefit in routine use).1 Universal Lynch syndrome tumor screening with MMR IHC ± MSI is recommended at diagnosis for all newly diagnosed endometrial cancers regardless of age (NCCN, SGO, ACOG).134 Survivorship care emphasizes lifestyle counseling (weight, exercise, diabetes, lipids), vasomotor and sexual health management, lymphedema surveillance, bone health after surgical menopause, distress screening, and age-appropriate other-cancer screening. USPSTF has issued no recommendation for endometrial cancer screening in asymptomatic average-risk women. Patients on active systemic therapy for metastatic / recurrent disease receive response imaging every 9–12 weeks and irAE labs with checkpoint inhibitor regimens.

Laboratory

  • Universal MMR IHC (± MLH1 methylation reflex) ± MSI — at diagnosis on all newly diagnosed endometrial cancers regardless of age or family history. Identify germline mutation carriers.134
  • Germline genetic counseling / testing — if MMR-deficient on IHC (not due to MLH1 methylation) or strong family history. Cascade testing for Lynch carriers.1
  • Serum CA-125 — only if elevated at baseline / diagnosis; otherwise NOT routine. Reserved for serous, clear cell, or any histology with elevated preop CA-125. TOTEM trial showed no benefit in routine use.12
  • Immune-related adverse event labs (TSH, LFTs, glucose, cortisol) — baseline and prior to each cycle on checkpoint inhibitor (pembrolizumab / dostarlimab).1

Imaging

  • Surveillance CT chest / abdomen / pelvis — every 6 months for first 3 years, then every 6–12 months for 2 additional years (consider) for stage III-IV, serous / clear cell, carcinosarcoma, or metastatic on systemic therapy. NOT routine for stage I, grade 1–2 disease.1
  • Vaginal / pelvic MRI or CT — as clinically indicated for symptoms or abnormal exam.1
  • Response imaging CT chest / abd / pelvis — every 2–3 cycles (≈ every 9–12 wk) during active systemic therapy for metastatic / recurrent disease.1

Physical examination

  • H&P with pelvic exam (speculum + bimanual) — every 3–6 mo for first 2–3 yr (stage III/IV or serous / clear cell every 3 mo); every 6 mo through yr 5; then annually. Risk-stratified to lower frequency for stage IA grade 1–2.12
  • Lymphedema surveillance + education — assess at each visit; refer to lymphedema therapy as needed (post pelvic ± para-aortic lymphadenectomy or pelvic RT).12
  • Vaginal cuff cytology / Pap — NOT recommended routinely (discontinued); symptom-driven evaluation only.12

Functional / surveys

  • Patient education on recurrence symptoms — at every visit. Vaginal bleeding, pelvic / abdominal pain, cough, weight loss, leg swelling.12
  • Lifestyle counseling — weight management, exercise, healthy diet — at each follow-up. Especially BMI ≥30, type 2 DM, metabolic syndrome (endometrial cancer is metabolically driven).1
  • Smoking cessation counseling — every visit.1
  • Cardiovascular risk management (BP, lipids, diabetes screening) — per primary care, at least annually.1
  • Vasomotor symptom management — as needed; non-hormonal preferred; hormone therapy case-by-case.1
  • Sexual health / vaginal health — assessed at each visit; moisturizers, dilators after vaginal brachytherapy / radiation; low-dose vaginal estrogen case-by-case.12
  • Bone health — DXA scan — baseline and per osteoporosis guidelines; consider in premenopausal women rendered menopausal by surgery / RT.1
  • Distress / depression / anxiety screening (NCCN Distress Thermometer) — at each visit.1
  • Age-appropriate cancer screening — breast, cervical per ACS / USPSTF, colorectal (accelerated to age 20–25 q1–2 yr if Lynch+).1

Notes

  • Vaginal cuff cytology. Historical practice (pre-2011) routinely included annual Pap of the vaginal cuff; current NCCN, SGO, ESGO, and ACOG all recommend against it — but it remains in some community settings.12
  • Surveillance imaging in high-risk disease. NCCN endorses routine CT every 6 months for 3 years in stage III-IV / serous / clear cell; ESMO and some SGO experts favor a more individualized, symptom-driven approach citing limited survival data. The 2025 SGO update incorporates molecular risk stratification (POLE-mutated, MMR-deficient, p53-abnormal, NSMP) to further refine imaging intensity.12
  • Lynch syndrome screening. NCCN and SGO now recommend universal MMR tumor testing at diagnosis regardless of age; older ACOG / SGO algorithms had offered alternative age-based or family-history-based pathways. Universal testing is now the dominant US standard.13
  • Post-treatment hormone therapy. ACOG / NAMS permit individualized systemic HT in early-stage low-grade survivors with severe vasomotor symptoms; NCCN takes a more cautious position favoring non-hormonal therapy first.1
  • ASCO endometrial-specific survivorship guideline. A Gynecologic Cancers Survivorship guideline is in development as of 2025; current ASCO guidance defers to NCCN / SGO.1

Urologic Cancer

ICD-10: C67.x (bladder), C64-C66 (kidney / renal pelvis / ureter) CCW prevalence (2022 Medicare FFS): 0.9% — rank 30 of 30 Category: Cancer Primary associations consulted: AUA / SUO, AUA / ASCO / SUO, NCCN, ASCO, USPSTF

CCW classifies bladder cancer and renal cell carcinoma together as "urologic cancer" (excluding prostate, which is condition #17). Both have distinct surveillance schedules and are addressed separately.

Bladder Cancer (NMIBC + MIBC)

At-a-glance schedule (Bladder Cancer)

Type Every visit Quarterly Every 6 months Annually Every 2–3 years As indicated
Labs — Urine cytology every 3–6 mo × 2 yr (intermediate / high-risk NMIBC); BCG response assessment cytology at 3 + 6 mo post-induction (high-risk on BCG); annual B12 starting 2–3 yr post-diversion (ileal conduit / neobladder); BMP / acid-base every 3–6 mo early postop, then annually (post-cystectomy diversions) Renal function (Cr / eGFR) + upper-tract imaging every 6 mo through yr 3 (post-cystectomy) Renal function + upper-tract imaging annually after yr 3 (post-cystectomy); B12 annually — Urinary biomarkers should NOT replace cystoscopy; selectively in surveillance
Imaging — Cystoscopy every 3–4 mo × 2 yr (high-risk NMIBC); every 3–6 mo × 2 yr (intermediate-risk); CT chest + CT / MRI abd / pelvis every 6–12 mo × 2–3 yr (post-cystectomy for MIBC) Cystoscopy every 6 mo yrs 3–4 (high / intermediate-risk NMIBC); CT chest + abd / pelvis annually after yr 3 (post-cystectomy) Cystoscopy annually after yr 4–5 (NMIBC); upper-tract imaging (CT / MR urography) every 1–2 yr (intermediate / high-risk NMIBC); cystoscopy first at 3–4 mo, then 6–9 mo, then annually (low-risk NMIBC) Shared decision-making to discontinue cystoscopy after 5 yr recurrence-free (low-risk NMIBC) Urethral wash cytology periodically if urethra retained (especially with prostatic urethral involvement); routine bladder cancer screening NOT recommended in asymptomatic adults (USPSTF I-statement)
Physical exam — — — — — Sexual / urinary function and ostomy / neobladder care
Functional / surveys — — — — — —

Summary (Bladder Cancer)

For NMIBC, the AUA / SUO 2024 Amendment recommends cystoscopy + cytology every 3–4 months × 2 years, every 6 months for years 3–4, then annually for high-risk disease, with upper-tract imaging (CT / MR urography) every 1–2 years; intermediate-risk uses every 3–6 month cystoscopy tapering to annual; low-risk requires cystoscopy at 3–4 months, 6–9 months, then annually, with no routine cytology or upper-tract imaging.1 Post-cystectomy MIBC patients (AUA / ASCO / SUO 2024) require chest + abd / pelvis CT or MRI every 6–12 months × 2–3 years then annually, plus lifelong metabolic monitoring (annual B12 for ileal-segment diversions, BMP for acid-base) and renal function / upper-tract surveillance.2

Renal Cell Carcinoma

At-a-glance schedule (Renal Cell Carcinoma)

Type Every visit Quarterly Every 6 months Annually Every 2–3 years As indicated
Labs — BMP / eGFR / CBC / LDH every 6 mo × 3 yr (stage II-III high-risk); response imaging on systemic therapy every 6–16 wk; irAE labs on ICI / TKI per regimen — BMP / Cr / eGFR / CBC / LDH annually (stage I); renal function annually (small renal mass on AS) — TSH / LFTs / BMP / CBC per ICI / TKI toxicity schedule; bone scan / brain MRI only if symptomatic (bone pain, ↑ alk phos, neurologic symptoms)
Imaging — Abdominal CT or MRI + chest CT every 3–6 mo × 3 yr (high-risk post-resection stage II-III); response imaging every 6–16 wk (stage IV on systemic therapy); abdominal imaging every 3–6 mo yr 1 (small renal mass on AS) — Abdominal imaging (CT / MRI; ultrasound acceptable after baseline) annually × 3 yr or longer (stage I post-resection); chest imaging (CXR or chest CT) annually × at least 5 yr; chest CT preferred annually × 5 yr (stage II-III high-risk after initial intensive window); abdominal imaging annually after yr 1 (small renal mass on AS) — —
Physical exam — H&P every 3–6 mo × 3 yr (stage II-III post-resection) — H&P annually (stage I); H&P annually to yr 5 (stage II-III); H&P + labs annually (stage I post-resection) — —
Functional / surveys — — — — — Renal-function preservation counseling (NSAID avoidance, contrast safety); active surveillance candidacy reassessment for small renal mass (growth <5 mm/yr; size <3–4 cm)

Summary (Renal Cell Carcinoma)

For RCC (AUA Renal Mass 2021; NCCN 2024–2025), stage I post-resection requires baseline imaging at 3–12 months then annually × 3+ years with annual chest imaging × 5 years; stage II-III requires every 3–6 month chest + abdominal CT × 3 years tapering to annual through year 5; small renal masses on active surveillance need imaging every 3–6 months in year 1 then every 6–12 months.34 Stage IV on systemic therapy requires response imaging every 6–16 weeks per regimen plus immunotherapy / TKI toxicity surveillance (TSH, LFTs, BMP, CBC).5

Combined Summary

US guidelines support intensive, risk-stratified surveillance for both major urologic cancers. NMIBC surveillance is anchored in cystoscopy + cytology every 3–4 months for years 1–2 in high-risk disease, tapering to annual after year 5; low-risk NMIBC is more permissive, with cytology and upper-tract imaging not routinely required. Post-cystectomy MIBC patients require chest + abd / pelvis CT or MRI every 6–12 months × 2–3 years then annually, plus annual B12 for ileal-segment diversions and renal-function / upper-tract surveillance. RCC post-resection cadence varies by stage (annual stage I; every 3–6 months × 3 years for stage II-III high-risk). Active surveillance for small renal mass requires baseline-to-frequent imaging early then annually. USPSTF gives bladder cancer screening in asymptomatic adults an "I" (insufficient evidence) statement.6

Notes

  • NMIBC low-risk intensity. AUA / SUO has progressively de-escalated low-risk surveillance (now allowing shared decision-making after 5 years recurrence-free). The 2024 AUA Amendment recommends against routine biomarker / cytology in low-risk patients with normal cystoscopy.1
  • Upper-tract imaging frequency. AUA recommends every 1–2 years for intermediate / high-risk NMIBC; NCCN / European guidance varies between 1-year and longer intervals based on prior upper-tract pathology.1
  • RCC active surveillance vs. intervention thresholds. AUA triggers intervention at tumor size >3–4 cm, growth >5 mm/year, or stage progression; NCCN is less prescriptive on absolute size cutoffs.34
  • Post-cystectomy B12 monitoring. Strongly recommended in clinical practice and AUA narrative guidance, but exact start time (immediate vs. 2–3 years postop) is not uniformly codified.2
  • ASCO RCC survivorship. No standalone ASCO RCC survivorship guideline exists; ASCO 2022 covers metastatic clear-cell RCC management.5
